But this relative wherein may have reference to the holiest of all, first mentioned, as well as to the ark; and then the meaning will be, that all those things were in the most holy place. Or if it have reference to the ark, he speaks of the things that were in it either before Solomon's time, or after, about the captivity. For though before and after Solomon other things might be in the ark besides the two tables of stone, yet in his time there was nothing else. See on Exod. 25.16.

*{which Moses put there at Horeb}* Horeb was a mountain in the desert of Sinai, where God appeared to Moses in the burning bush, Exod. 3.1,2. and where the Israelites abode in the third month after their coming out of Egypt: whereupon it was called Mount Sinai, Exod. 19.1,11. On this mountain God appeared in glorious majesty and great terror, Exod. 29.16, &c. and on it he delivered the law written in two tables, Deut. 4.15. And while Israel remained at this mountain Moses put that law into the ark, Deut. 10.5. There stood God when he gave them water out of the rock, Exod. 17.6. which is called *the spiritual rock*, 1 Cor. 10.4. It is in one of these respects called *the mount of God*, Exod. 3.1. where this title is given it by anticipation. See ch. 19.8.

*{when}* Or, *where*.

*{the LORD made}* Heb. *cut*. This word cut is in the Hebrew originally used without expression of covenant, but yet intends the making of a covenant: and therefore our English to make up the sense does add the word covenant, as here, 2 Chron. 5.10. 1 Sam. 20.16. & 22.8. The reason of using this word cut to that purpose was because they were wont in making a covenant to cut a beast in sunder for confirmation thereof, Gen. 15.10,18. Jer. 34.18.

*{a covenant with the children of Israel}* The law is called *a covenant* because obedience therein was required on their part, and a blessing promised on God's part.

*{when they came out of the land of Egypt}* Mention is made of this circumstance, because then God began to make them a free people, and a peculiar treasure to himself, Exod. 19.5. and also because that deliverance out of Egypt was an especial type of our redemption from bondage under Satan.

**V. 10.** *{And it came to pass when the priests were come out of the holy place}* By the holy place is meant that part of the temple which was next to the most holy, where the priests executed their function, v. 11. Exod. 28.43.

*{that the cloud}* This cloud was an extraordinary one ordained for manifestation of God's presence, Levit. 16.2. in a cloud did God oft manifest his presence, Exod. 13.21. & 14.19,24. & 19.9,16. Numb. 9.15, &c. In the next verse it is called *the glory of the Lord*, to show that his majesty is incomprehensible, and the brightness thereof such as, if it were not clouded over, would dazzle men, yea, and utterly confound them, Matt. 17.5. Act. 1.9. Rev. 1.7.

*{filled the house of the LORD}* Throughout, in all the parts of it from one end to another.

**V. 11.** *{So that the priests could not stand to minister because of the cloud}* They could not continue or abide in the house where the cloud was: And that first, for reverence' sake: Secondly, by reason of a great amazement, 1 Chron. 21.16,30. Thirdly, for want of convenient light. If it be said that this phrase following *the glory of the Lord* implies light in the cloud, I answer, 1. It does not necessarily imply light. For v. 12. it is said that *the Lord would dwell in thick darknesse*. And though glory were set forth therein, yet it might be, because the cloud implied an incomprehensibleness thereof. 2. It might be a great brightness, and yet not give convenient light to do any work by. 3. It might dazzle them too much. The like is noted Exod. 40.35.

*{for the glory of the LORD had filled the house of the LORD}* This shows that the cloud set forth God's glory. This the Lord thus did, to sanctify to himself this place by this visible sign, as Exod. 40.34. Lev. 9.23,24. and to show his approbation of all that was done: as he did by other signs at other times, Gen. 4.4. 1 King. 18.38. See 2 Chron. 7.1.

**V. 12.** *{Then spake Solomon}* Solomon discerning this manifestation of God's glory to be a sign of his gracious presence, quickly makes known as much.

*{The LORD said that he would dwell in the thick darkness}* 2 Chr. 6.1. Levit. 16.2. Psal. 18.11,12. & 97.2. In that God oft manifested himself in darkness, it is as much as if he had said he would dwell in darkness. Hereby is meant that man cannot possibly discern his excellency. Who can directly behold the sun shining forth in its brightness? But yet through a mist or a thin cloud we may look upon it.

**V. 13.** *{I have surely built}* Heb. *Building I have built*. The Hebrews place much emphasis in doubling verbs. And thereby they set out, 1. Certainty, Exod. 3.7. 2. Abundance, Gen. 22.17. 3. Diligence, Ex. 21.19. 4. A full or thorough doing of a thing, 1 Sam. 26.25. 5. Celerity and speed, 1 Sam. 27.1. 6. Finishing or perfecting a thing: as here, *I have perfectly finished*.

*{thee}* Or, *for thee*. Solomon directs his speech to God, to testify the great devotion of his soul.

*{an house to dwell in}* A temple to manifest your presence in. Men use so to do in their houses.

*{a settled place}* Not to be removed, as the tabernacle was, See v. 4.

*{for thee to abide in}* To be as one that continually remains in a place. To be there ready on all occasions to resolve such cases as should be propounded, to hear such prayers as should be made, to grant such blessings as should be craved, and to accept such sacrifices as should be offered up, and such services as should be performed in that house.

*{for ever}* Or, *ages*. The Hebrew word translated *ever* is diversely taken, as for ages or time past, Eccl. 1.10. so for ages or time to come. In this latter sense it is used, 1. Indefinitely, for a long time, Dan. 3.9. Josh. 8.28. II. For the period and accomplishment of a set determinate time; as the year of jubilee was. Compare Ex. 21.6. with Levit. 25.40. III. For the continuance of one's life. Compare 1 Sam. 1.11. with v. 22. IV. For so long as the conditions annexed to a thing are observed, 1 Sam. 2.30. V. For the continuance of a state, Numb. 10.8. VI. Till the truth of a type be exhibited. Compare Gen. 17.13. with Gal. 5.2. VII. For an everlasting continuance, Luk. 1.33. VIII. For that which never had beginning, nor ever shall have end, but is properly eternal, Psal. 90.2. Here it is taken in the fourth respect, till conditions were observed, (Note ch. 9.7,8,9. & 2 King. 25.9.) and also in the sixth respect, till the Messiah, the true temple, should be exhibited, Heb. 8.1,2.

**V. 14.** *{And the king turned his face about}* Solomon in the former verse directed his speech to God, and thereupon turned his face to the most holy place, where the ark, the evidence of God's presence, was: Yea, he had turned himself that way to behold the priests setting of the ark in his place. But now directing his speech to the people, he turns about, because they were behind him.

*{and blessed}* This has relation both to his praying to the Lord for all blessings on them, as Numb. 6.23. Psal. 118.26. and also to his praising the Lord for his mercy to them, as v. 55,56. Indeed by virtue of special function it belonged to the priests to bless the people, Numb. 6.23. Deut. 21.5. So did Melchizedek, Gen. 14.19. Heb. 7.6. and Aaron, Levit. 9.22. and a special promise was made to the priests' blessing, Num. 6.27. Yet others also blessed, by wishing good, and praying for it: as inferiors, Ruth 2.4. equals, Ps. 118.26. superiors; especially such as had charge over others; as parents, Gen. 31.55. Masters of families, 2 Sam. 6.20. Governors of subjects, Josh. 22.6. 1 Chr. 16.2.

*{all the congregation of Israel}* Namely all that were come to Jerusalem, and there present.

*{stood}* This sets out their gesture even while the priests were doing their office in the temple, but especially when the king turned and spoke to them. They gave evidence hereby of their reverence, attention and observation.

**V. 15.** *{And he said}* The king uttered the words following.

*{Blessed be}* Or, *Praised be*. All that blessing wherewith creatures can bless God, is thankfully to acknowledge what he does, and to praise him for it, Ps. 115.17,18.

*{the LORD God of Israel}* Lord sets out his sovereignty; God of Israel, his special relation to his church.

*{which spake with his mouth}* A Hebrew pleonasm, or redundancy of speech, as *to see with ones eyes*, Deut. 3.27. *to hear with his ears*, 2 Sam. 7.22. This is attributed to God after the manner of man. It was by the ministry of Nathan that God spoke this, 2 Sam. 7.6. For prophets are God's mouth, Exod. 4.15. Isa. 3.16. 2 Cor. 13.3.

*{to David}* Concerning the building of a temple by his son.

*{my father}* For his father's honor and his own also he mentions this relation. For it was an honor to be David's son, and an honor to be Solomon's father.

*{and hath with his hand fulfilled it, saying}* Such promises as divine providence brings to pass by man's ministry, God is said to fulfil with his hand, because all manner of instruments, men and others, are in the hand of God's providence.

**V. 16.** *{Since the day that I brought forth my people Israel out of Egypt}* From the time that I made Israel a distinct, peculiar, and free people. They were before bondslaves in Egypt, therefore oft put in mind of their deliverance thence.

*{I chose no city}* As I never determined nor such a thing, so neither did I ever appoint any person to build a house, or any place where it should be built. So Psal. 132.13.

*{Out of all the tribes of Israel}* Or, *out of any of them*.

*{to build an house}* This act has relation to the word city before mentioned; so either the word city is synecdochically put for the inhabitants thereof, or the act of building is to be taken passively, *q.d.* I chose no city to have a house built therein.

*{that my name might be there}* This intends that the temple was built to be dedicated to God for a place of his worship, and for service to be performed to him therein, Deut. 12.11.

*{but I chose David}* Till I chose David, and manifested my purpose concerning him, 1 Sam. 13.14. & 16.1,12.

*{to be over my people Israel}* To be their king, and a governor over them. He means not only a choice of David's person, but also of his stock and posterity, 2 Sam. 7.15,27.

**V. 17.** *{And it was in the heart}* Or, *mind*. He purposed and intended to build, &c. So 2 Sam. 7.3. 1 Chron. 22.7.

*{of David my father}* David being a pious man, would herein testify his zeal of God's glory.

*{to build an house}* A holy temple, a place of worship.

*{for the name}* See v. 16. & ch. 5.3.

*{of the LORD God of Israel}* See v. 15.

**V. 18.** *{And the LORD said unto David my father}* God is ready to direct such as through piety intend that which is amiss.

*{Whereas it was in thine heart to build an house to my name, thou didst well that it was in thine heart}* This is a phrase of approbation: And that God approved this purpose of David, is evident by the promise he made thereupon, 2 Sam. 7.11, &c. This instance shows that a man may without sin intend to do that which God has not purposed shall be done.

**V. 19.** *{Nevertheless thou shalt not build the house}* See ch. 5.3.

*{but thy son that shall come forth out of thy loins}* One of your own sons, which you yourself shall beget. See 1 Chron. 22.9. 2 Sam. 7.12.

*{he shall build the house unto my name}* See on v. 17.

**V. 20.** *{And the LORD hath performed the word that he spake}* See v. 15.

*{and I am risen up in the room of David my father}* I succeed him.

*{and sit on the throne of Israel}* Am king over the people of Israel, my father being dead.

*{as the LORD promised}* 2 Sam. 7.12. 1 Chron. 28.5.

*{and have built an house for the name of the LORD God of Israel}* See v. 13,15,17.

**V. 21.** *{And I have set there a place for the ark}* That it may there abide and not be removed, as formerly. See v. 6.

*{wherein is the covenant of the LORD}* See v. 6. ch. 3.15. The two tables of stone, v. 9. wherein the law, that God gave as a covenant to his people, was written, were in this ark.

*{which he made with our fathers, when he brought them out of the land of Egypt}* See v. 9.

**V. 22.** *{And Solomon stood}* Upon a brazen scaffold, 2 Chr. 6.13. So did other kings, 2 King. 11.14. & 23.3. After that Solomon had there stood a while, he kneeled down, v. 54. and spread forth his hands. Standing sets forth the erection of his spirit, and steadfastness of his faith in God; Kneeling, the humiliation of his soul, and acknowledgment of God's sovereignty; Spreading forth his hands, an expectation of blessing, and readiness to receive it. These were usual gestures of prayer in those times, Mark 11.25. Isa. 1.15. Dan. 6.10.

*{before the altar of the LORD}* 2 Chron. 6.12. He means the altar of burnt offering. For he himself was in the court, but at the door of that place where the altar stood: And he turned himself towards the most holy place, because he made his prayer to God. Of this altar see 2 Chr. 4.1.

*{in the presence of all the congregation}* See v. 2.

*{and spread forth his hands toward heaven}* Testifying thereby that his hope was in heaven, and that from thence he expected to be heard.

**V. 23.** *{And he said}* Here he begins his solemn prayer at the dedication of the temple.

*{LORD God of Israel}* See v. 15.

*{there is no God like thee}* In the beginning of his prayer he sets out the excellency of his God, the only true God, on whom he called, to work the greater reverence, yea, and confidence in his hearers, and every way to fit his spirit the better to call upon such a God.

*{in heaven above, or on earth beneath}* Some feigned false gods to themselves in heaven, as the sun, moon, planets, and other stars, 2 King. 17.16. & 23.5. Others had their gods on earth, Judg. 10.6. & 17.5. 2 Chr. 13.8.

*{who keepest covenant}* Herein God manifests his faithfulness, Psal. 89.33,34. Dan. 9.4.

*{and mercy}* Faithfulness and mercy in God are oft joined together, Ps. 36.5. & 89.1,24. For it is mercy that moves God to enter into covenant with man, and to make it good which is a part of faithfulness.

*{with thy servants that walk before thee with all their heart}* God's covenants have their provisos, which set down conditions to be observed on man's part; that by the performance thereof, man might testify his thankfulness to God, and also gain assurance to himself, and give evidence to others that he has a right to the covenant. See ch. 2.4. & 6.12.

**V. 24.** *{Who hast kept}* Or, *begun to keep*. For he prays for a further keeping of promise, v. 25.

*{with thy servant David my father}* See ch. 3.6.

*{that thou promisedst him}* He means in special the promise mentioned 2 Sam. 7.12,13, &c.

*{thou spakest also with thy mouth, and hast fulfilled it with thine hand}* See v. 15.

*{as it is this day}* For then was Solomon king, and then he had finished God's house, which were special promises here intended. See ch. 3.6.

**V. 25.** *{Therefore now, LORD God of Israel, keep with thy servant David my father that thou promisedst him, saying}* Solomon here prays for God's continuing of mercy to him, and his posterity, according to the extent of God's promise, *I will stablish the throne of his Kingdom for ever*, 2 Sam. 7.13.

*{there shall not fail thee a man in my sight, to sit on the throne of Israel}* Heb. *there shall not be cut off unto thee a man from my sight*. chap. 2.4. 2 Sam. 7.12. His meaning is, There shall ever be some lineally descending from David to sit on his throne. See 2 Chr. 21.7. This was especially fulfilled in Christ, Luk. 1.32,33.

*{so that}* Heb. *only if*. See v. 23.

*{thy children take heed to their way, that they walk before me}* Children who expect benefit from promises made to their fathers, must carefully observe the conditions of those promises.

*{as thou hast walked before me}* Pious practices of parents are a pattern to children.

**V. 26.** *{And now, O God of Israel, let thy word (I pray thee) be verified which thou spakest to thy servant David my father}* The accomplishment of what God has promised may and ought to be prayed for, Deut. 1.11. 2 Sam. 7.28,29. 2 Chron. 1.9. Dan. 9.2. For prayer is a means sanctified of God for accomplishment of what he has purposed and promised, Ezek. 36.36,37. and it is an evidence of our hearty desire thereof, yea, and of God's faithfulness.

**V. 27.** *{But will God indeed dwell on the earth?}* An emphatical admiration of God's goodness in vouchsafing his presence in any place on earth, 2 Chr. 2.6. To speak properly, God, being infinite, cannot be contained within any compass, as the words following show. See Jer. 23.23,24. Act. 7.49.

*{Behold, the heaven, and the heaven of heavens cannot contain thee}* That which compasses about all the other heavens, and is the most spacious place that ever was created. It is called *the highest*, Luk. 2.14. and *the third heaven*, 2 Cor. 12.2. He multiplies phrases, as, *heaven, and the heaven of heavens*, the more to set out the immensity of God. Such Hebraisms are a great amplification of a matter. See on Gen. 1.8. Lev. 2.3. Deut. 10.14. Psal. 148.4.

*{How much less this house that I have builded}* Hereby he shows he did not think that God could properly be contained in any place.

**V. 28.** *{Yet have thou respect unto the prayer}* Though you be not contained in this house, yet manifest so much respect unto it, as it may be known to be a monument and evidence of your gracious presence, and of your readiness to hear.

*{of thy servant}* He means himself. It is a title, as here used, of reverence and confidence. See Psal. 116.16.

*{and to his supplication}* Prayer imports petition for good things; supplication, a desire of pardon of sin, and of remitting evils.

*{O LORD my God}* Lord, sets out God's sovereignty; My God, the Lord's special respect to him. The former is to humble, and work reverence; the latter to raise up, and to work confidence in him.

*{to hearken unto the cry}* Cry notes the earnestness of desire, Exod. 14.15. Esth. 9.31.

*{and to the prayer which thy servant prayeth before thee to day}* Under this word prayer he does in general comprise all the particulars which in this chapter are prayed for.

**V. 29.** *{That thine eyes may be open}* Another phrase applied to God after the manner of men, as v. 24. The meaning is, that God would show that he takes special notice of all things done in that house, by hearing all manner of prayers in it, or towards it. See Ps. 33.16. & 34.15. The like may be said of God's hearkening, or being attent with his ears, 2 Chr. 6.40.

*{towards this house}* For it was an especial type of Christ, Joh. 2.19,21.

*{night and day}* Continually.

*{even toward the place of which thou hast said}* Deut. 12.11. God's promise was the ground of his prayer.

*{My name shall be there}* See v. 16.

*{that thou mayest hearken unto the prayer which thy servant shall make toward this place}* Or, *in this place*. See v. 44.

**V. 30.** *{And hearken thou unto the supplication of thy servant}* He intends the same thing he did before, v. 28,29. But to manifest the earnestness of his desire, he inculcates it again and again.

*{and of thy people Israel}* This he adds both in relation to them that were then present, to testify his persuasion of their assent to his prayer; and also to others in that present age, or in future generations: and he desires that God would from age to age hear them all so praying.

*{when they shall pray toward this place}* Or, *in this place*.

*{and hear thou}* Again he inculcates the same thing.

*{in heaven thy dwelling-place}* The heaven can no more contain God than the temple could. But because God's glory is most manifested in heaven and from heaven, heaven is said to be God's dwelling-place, Ps. 113.5. & 123.1. Mat. 6.9.

*{and when thou hearest, forgive}* Many of the particulars are in case of wrong, or confession of sins, or for removing judgments: In all which respects pardon of sin was necessary: yea, there is in us such a spring and sink of corruption, as in all things, even the best that we do, we need pardon. See v. 34.

**V. 31.** *{If any man trespass against his neighbour}* Or *be suspected to have done wrong to any*, as Exod. 22.8, &c. Numb. 5.14, &c. For in apparent wrongs, or such as might be proved by witnesses, the wrongdoer was not to put to an oath, but to be punished.

*{and an oath be laid upon him}* Heb. *and he require an oath of him*. i.e. an oath of execration against himself, in case he swear falsely, Num. 5.21, &c. Job 31.7,8,9. Psalm 7.4,5. Such an oath does ordinarily strike a man with such terror as he dare not take it falsely, if at least he have any fear of God in him.

*{to cause him to swear}* Namely, by calling upon the judge, as Exod. 22.8. or a priest, as Numb. 5.14,15. to put him to his oath.

*{and the oath come before thine altar in this house}* The altar being a sign of God's presence there, thither was he brought, that the greater fear might be worked in him, Numb. 5.16. With us he that takes a public oath, lays his hand on a Bible, to work in him the greater fear. The altar here was the burnt-offering altar, whereof see 2 Chron. 4.1.

**V. 32.** *{Then hear thou in heaven}* Though you be in heaven, yet show that you hear things on earth.

*{and do, and judge thy servants}* So order matters as the truth may be found out, and judgment answerably given.

*{condemning the wicked}* For this is a part of judgment.

*{to bring his way upon his head}* i.e. his just desert, Ezek. 9.10. Or such measure as he would have meted unto others. The law of returning like for like may be here intended. See chap. 2.32.

*{and justifying the righteous}* Whether it be accuser or accused. This is another part of justice, Prov. 17.15.

*{to give him according to his righteousness}* Though God do not reward men for their righteousness, as a cause of their reward, yet he does order his reward according to men's deeds. Psal. 62.12. Rom. 2.6.

**V. 33.** *{When thy people Israel be smitten down before the enemy}* Or, *overcome*. Mention is made of four cases in regard of enemies: I. When enemies smite them down in the field; II. When enemies besiege them, v. 37. III. When God sends them out against enemies, v. 44. IV. When enemies carry them into captivity, v. 46.

*{because they have sinned against thee}* Sin provokes God to give his people over into their enemies' hand, Levit. 26.17.

*{and shall turn again to thee}* Repentance is a means both of preventing, Jon. 3.10. and also of removing a judgment, 1 Sam. 7.3.

*{and confess thy name}* By God's name he means God's justice in giving them over to the enemy, see Josh. 7.9,19.

*{and pray}* Prayer of penitents is prevalent, Judg. 10.15,16.

*{and make supplication unto thee}* See v. 28.

*{in this house}* Or, *towards*. See v. 29.

**V. 34.** *{Then hear thou in heaven}* See v. 32.

*{and forgive the sin of thy people Israel}* Sin must be forgiven before a judgment can be removed. If a judgment should be removed, and sin not forgiven, it would bring but small comfort. See 2 Chr. 7.14.

*{and bring them again unto the land}* The land here meant was their proper inheritance, wherein they might most safely, quietly and comfortably abide. This mercy of bringing them again to the land is indefinitely to be taken, as when they were removed from one tribe to another, as 2 Chron. 28.8. or forced from their own house, as 1 Sam. 31.7. or forced to fly before enemies, as Josh. 7.7,8. or taken prisoners, as Numb. 21.1. or carried captives into another land, as Jer. 39.9.

*{which thou gavest unto their fathers}* Gen. 13.15. Ex. 6.8. Josh. 21.43. God's gift is a good title.

**V. 35.** *{When heaven}* i.e. the lowest heaven, which is the air, and the middle region thereof, where the clouds are, ch. 18.45. Ps. 147.8. Mat. 24.30.

*{is shut up, and there is no rain}* When clouds let down rain, the heavens are said to be opened, Deut. 28.12. and to be shut up when there is no rain, Deut. 11.17. yea, they are said to be as iron, which melts not, when there is no rain, Levit. 26.19. Heaven is here resembled to a vessel or barrel full of water, which being shut, no water can come out of it, Job 38.37. God is as a gardener, who carrying a waterpot in his hand, lets the water run out when and where he pleases, Amos 4.7.

*{because they have sinned against thee}* See v. 33.

*{if they pray towards this place}* See v. 28,29.

*{and confess thy name}* See v. 33.

*{and turn from their sin}* See v. 33.

*{when thou afflictest them}* Affliction is a means of turning sinners to God. And repentance worked by affliction may be sound and acceptable, Psal. 119.67,71. Hos. 5.15. 2 Chron. 33.12,13.

**V. 36.** *{Then hear thou in heaven}* See v. 32.

*{and forgive the sin of thy servants, and of thy people Israel}* See v. 34. The people of Israel were by profession God's servants.

*{that thou shouldest teach them}* By the affliction before mentioned. Or, *when thou shalt have taught them*, as 2 Chr. 6.27. and so they have learned the end and use of your judgments, Isa. 26.9. Such things cannot be learned without God's teaching, whereby the mind is enlightened, and the will made pliable, Psal. 119.33, &c. Isa. 54.13.

*{the good way wherein they should walk}* i.e. the right course prescribed in God's word. It's called good, because pleasing to God, and profitable to them who walk therein.

*{and give rain}* As prayer is to be made for removing evils, Judg. 10.15. so for obtaining good things, Joel 2.19,23.

*{upon thy land which thou hast given thy people Israel for an inheritance}* See v. 34.

**V. 37.** *{If there be in the land famine}* This is one of God's heavy judgments, 2 Sam. 24.13. Ezek. 5.16.

*{if there be pestilence}* Under this is comprised every infecting destroying disease, 2 Sam. 24.15.

*{blasting}* By the east wind, which smiting green ears of corn makes them wither, Gen. 41.6.

*{mildew}* A dew that falling on corn turns the grain into noisome dust or powder. Blasting and mildew are oft joined together, as Deut. 28.22. Amos 4.9. Hag. 2.17.

*{locusts}* That is a fly with long legs, which wastes corn; Ex. 10.4,5. Deut. 28.42.

*{or if there be caterpillars}* Certain worms which lie upon corn or other green plants, and consume them, Psal. 105.34,35. See Levit. 11.22. These four last are ordinary causes of famine.

*{if their enemies besiege them}* Another cause of famine.

*{in the land of their cities}* Or, *in the cities of their land*; or, *jurisdiction*. This presupposes an enemy's entering into their land.

*{whatsoever plague}* The Hebrew word signifies *a stroke*. It is translated *stripes*, 2 Sam. 7.14. It is put for any kind of judgment, Gen. 12.17. Exod. 9.14. Deut. 28.59,60. Jer. 19.8. 2 Chron. 21.14.

*{whatsoever sickness there be}* There are very many kinds of sickness. Therefore this indefinite particle whatsoever is set before it.

**V. 38.** *{What prayer and supplication soever be made}* See v. 28.

*{by any man}* Singly alone.

*{or by all thy people Israel}* Jointly together.

*{which shall know every man the plague of his own heart}* The sin by which his own heart and conscience is smitten, as 2 Sam. 24.10. and thereby moved to pray.

*{and spread forth his hands}* See v. 22.

*{towards this house}* See v. 29.

**V. 39.** *{Then hear thou in heaven thy dwelling-place}* See v. 30,32.

*{and forgive}* See v. 34.

*{and do}* What in wisdom you see meet.

*{and give to every man according to his ways}* According to the present course which he takes, namely of repentance.

*{whose heart thou knowest}* Whether it be sincere or no, Psal. 139.1,2, &c.

*{for thou, even thou only knowest the hearts of all the children of men}* It is God's prerogative, and one of his incommunicable properties, to search the heart, and to know the inward motions and thoughts thereof, Jer. 17.10. Act. 1.24.

**V. 40.** *{That they may fear thee}* God's mercy in removing judgment does most kindly work fear, Psal. 130.4.

*{all the days that they live}* True grace continues all the time of a man's life, as Jer. 32.39.

*{in the land which thou gavest unto our fathers}* See v. 34.

**V. 41.** *{Moreover, concerning a stranger that is not of thy people Israel}* All that were not born Jews, were by them accounted strangers, though converted to the Jewish religion, Exod. 12.48. Such strangers as were circumcised might approach as near to the altar as born Jews, Exod. 12.44. Other strangers hearing of the God of Israel, Ruth 1.6. of his great works, Josh. 2.10. of his righteous laws, Deut. 4.6,7. and of his holy house, Isai. 56.7. yea and of this prayer which Solomon had made for God's presence in it, might be moved to come to it, and there to pray.

*{but cometh out of a far country}* God's name was everywhere far and near spread abroad, which made many that were in remote places come to Jerusalem, Matth. 2.1. & 12.42. Act. 6.7. & 8.27.

*{for thy name sake}* To testify his high esteem of your great name, to worship and praise you, having heard of you so much as he knows and believes you to be the only true God, Exod. 12.38. & 18.11,12. Josh. 2.9. See ch. 5.1.

**V. 42.** *{For they shall hear of thy great name}* Sundry of the heathen came on sundry occasions to the Jews, Jer. 27.3. and the Jews on sundry occasions went to other nations. Thus might strangers hear of God's great name.

*{and of thy strong hand, and of thy stretched-out arm}* These phrases are applied to God, after the manner of man, as other like, ver. 24,30. The strength and power of a man is manifested by his hand and arm; and when he would do some great matter by his arm, he stretches it out; the mighty power of God is therefore set out by these metaphors, as Ps. 136.12. Jer. 32.17. See 2 King. 17.36.

*{when he shall come and pray}* Devotion so works on men's spirits, as it makes them go out of their own land to worship God, Act. 8.27.

*{towards this house}* For the uncircumcised might not come so near the temple as the Jews might, Act. 21.29.

**V. 43.** *{Hear thou in heaven thy dwelling-place}* See v. 32.

*{and do according to all that the stranger calleth to thee for}* This particle all has a large extent, yet it is to be restrained to things lawful. That extent gives evidence of God's bounty, and is an encouragement for all, in all their needs, to seek blessing of God.

*{that all people of the earth}* God's bounty to some is a means to draw many to him.

*{may know thy name, to fear thee}* Knowledge of God is an especial means of working a due fear of him in man's heart.

*{as do thy people Israel}* Who from their childhood have been instructed in such mysteries as set God forth. And such as are so instructed, ought to be a good example to others.

*{that this house which I have builded, is called by thy name}* Heb. *thy name is called upon this house*. In that it was called *the Temple of the Lord* and *a house of God*, it was called by God's name. As the sons of Joseph had Israel's name named on them, in that they were called children of Israel, and Israelites, Gen. 48.16.

**V. 44.** *{If thy people go to battle against their enemy}* In a just cause. For in unjust war good success from God can be no more expected than in oppression, robbery, murder, and other like evils.

*{whithersoever thou shalt send them}* God's warrant for the war intended is a good ground of faith in God's assistance, and of praying for good success.

*{and shall pray unto the LORD toward the city}* Heb. *the way of the city*. When they cannot come to it; testifying thereby that all their confidence is placed on the true God, whose presence is in an especial manner manifested in the temple.

*{which thou hast chosen}* Jerusalem is in especial manner meant, ch. 11.13,32,36. See Dan. 6.10. God is said to have chosen it, in that he preferred it before all other cities to build his house therein, Psal. 78.67,68,69.

*{and toward the house that I have built for thy name}* See v. 20. This is the reason why God is said to choose that city, namely because his temple was there situated, 2 King. 21.7. & 23.27.

**V. 45.** *{Then hear thou in heaven}* See v. 32.

*{their prayer, and their supplication}* See v. 28.

*{and maintain their cause}* Or, *their just and righteous cause, *by giving them good success, Ps. 94. & 140.12. See v. 49.

**V. 46.** *{If they sin against thee}* They whom he styled Gods people, v. 44. even these are subject to sin. For among these some are hypocrites, and have no true grace at all in them: and in all of them flesh remains.

*{for there is no man that sinneth not}* 2 Chron. 6.36. Eccl. 7.20. 1 Joh. 1.8,10. It was Christ's prerogative, and his alone, to be here on earth without sin, Heb. 4.15. 1 Pet. 2.22,24.

*{and thou be angry with them}* The sins even of God's people provoke his anger, 2 Sam. 24.1. Numb. 11.1,10.

*{and deliver them to the enemy}* God makes enemies of his people executioners of his anger on them, to make them fear to provoke God's anger. For enemies will do to the uttermost that they can, Zach. 1.15.

*{so that they carry them away captives, unto the land of the enemy}* Captives are to be made slaves to the enemies: and this is counted the lowest and basest condition that any in this world can be brought unto, Exod. 12.29. Lam. 1.3,5. Jeremiah has set out the misery of captivity to the life, Lam. 5.1, &c.

*{far}* Babylon was far from Jerusalem: yet thither were the Jews carried captive, Jer. 40.1.

*{or near}* Samaria was near to Jerusalem, and to Samaria were the inhabitants of Jerusalem carried captive, 2 Chron. 28.8.

**V. 47.** *{Yet if they shall bethink themselves}* Heb. *bring back to their heart*. Or, *call their sins to mind*.

*{in the land whither they were carried captives}* God is with his people even in captivity, observing their disposition.

*{and repent}* See v. 33.

*{and make supplication unto thee}* See v. 28.

*{in the land of them that carried them captives, saying}* God everywhere hears prayers: Pray therefore everywhere, 1 Tim. 2.1. See Dan. 6.10.

*{We have sinned}* Penitent confession of sin is a ready way to obtain pardon and deliverance, Prov. 28.13. 1 Joh. 1.9. 2 Sam. 12.13. Psal. 32.5.

*{and have done perversely, we have committed wickedness}* This varying and multiplying of phrases makes much to the aggravation of sin, and shows that sinners ought rather to aggravate their sins before God, who knows them to the full, than extenuate them, Ezr. 9.6. Neh. 9.26. Dan. 9.5, &c.

**V. 48.** *{And so return unto thee with all their heart, and with all their soul}* These two phrases set out a sincere, sound, and thorough repentance. See ch. 2.4.

*{in the land of their enemy which led them away captive}* See v. 47.

*{and pray unto thee toward their land, which thou gavest unto their fathers, the city which thou hast chosen, and the house which I have built for thy name}* See v. 44. These several phrases, *their land*, *this city*, and *the house*, intend in substance one and the same thing. He mentions the land, because the city which God chose was in it; the city, because the house of the Lord was therein; the house, because the Lord was, in sundry external evidences of his presence, therein. Besides, the land here meant was that which God of old had promised to their fathers, Deut. 19.8. Josh. 23.5. and in that respect was called *the land of promise*, Heb. 11.9. So as their praying towards this land brought to their mind God's promise, whereby their faith might be strengthened.

**V. 49.** *{Then hear thou their prayer, and their supplication, in heaven thy dwelling-place}* See v. 28,30,32.

*{and maintain their cause}* Or, *right*. Take part with your people that repent and pray against the unjust oppression of their enemies, Isai. 47.6. Zach. 1.15.

**V. 50.** *{And forgive thy people that have sinned against thee}* See v. 34,36.

*{and all their transgressions wherein they have transgressed against thee}* See v. 47.

*{and give them compassion before them that carried them captives, that they may have compassion on them}* i.e. procure pity, grace, and favor for them: Turn the heart of their enemies towards them. This prayer shows that the hearts of enemies are in God's hands, to turn them as it pleases him, Gen. 39.21. & 43.14. Examples Ez. 7.6. Neh. 2.2. Hest. 2.9,17. & 6.10. & 10.2,3. Dan. 1.9,19. & 2.48,49. Ps. 106.46.

**V. 51.** *{For they be thy people}* Israel was the people whom God chose, set apart and severed from all the world, to be a peculiar people to himself, that he might bestow on them his choicest blessings, and that they might serve him, Exod. 19.4,5,6. Deut. 7.6, &c. and 10.15. This special relation is pleaded as a ground of God's showing mercy to them, Exod. 32.11,12. Neh. 1.10. Isai. 64.9.

*{and thine inheritance}* Israel is said to be God's inheritance, because that people were to God as is to man his inheritance. I. God bought them, Deut. 32.6. II. He made them his own forever, Gen. 17.7. III. He has his people in high account, and prefers them before all the world besides, Exod. 19.5. IV. He fences and protects them from danger, Ps. 89.18. Zech. 1.5. V. He sows and plants in them sanctifying and saving graces, Isai. 60.21. & 61.3. Mat. 13.14. VI. He continues to dung and water them, that is, to use all means whereby they may grow in grace, Luk. 13.8. Isa. 27.3. 1 Cor. 3.6. VII. He expects fruit most of all from them, Isai. 5.2. Luk. 13.6.

*{which thou broughtest out of Egypt}* See v. 9,16.

*{from the midst of the furnace of iron}* This phrase sets forth their hard and cruel oppression in Egypt. See Deut. 4.20. Isai. 48.10. A furnace to melt iron in, uses to be the hottest. See Jer. 11.4. It also implies, that God cast them into that furnace, to make them pliable to such ordinances as he should give them; as iron cast into a furnace and melted, may be cast into any fashion.

**V. 52.** *{That thine eyes may be open unto the supplication}* See v. 29.

*{of thy servant}* He means himself. See v. 59.

*{and unto the supplication of thy people Israel}* Prayer may be made, as for ourselves in particular, so for others also jointly with us.

*{to hearken unto them in all they call for unto thee}* See v. 28. Under this general particle all he comprises only things lawful and meet to be asked. All such may be prayed for, and all such God can and will readily give.

**V. 53.** *{For thou didst separate them from among all the people of the earth}* i.e. God did choose and call them to be a peculiar people unto himself, Exod. 19.5. Deut. 7.6. & 14.2.

*{to be thine inheritance}* Deut. 9.26,29. See v. 51.

*{as thou spakest by the hand of Moses}* Exod. 19.5. Deut. 32.8. By the hand of Moses he means the ministry of Moses. For the hand is that part of the body whereby men usually do, practice and execute things committed to them; and therefore in Hebrew it is put for any kind of ministry. Here it is put for the tongue or mouth. So v. 56.

*{thy servant}* See ch. 3.6. Josh. 1.1,2.

*{when thou broughtest our fathers out of Egypt}* See v. 9,16.

*{O Lord GOD}* See v. 28.

**V. 54.** *{And it was so, that when Solomon had made an end of praying}* Solomon held up his spirit till he had by prayer made known what he thought meet to be desired about the house he built.

*{all this prayer and supplication unto the LORD}* See v. 28.

*{he rose from before the altar of the LORD}* See v. 22.

*{from kneeling on his knees}* While he blessed the people, and blessed the Lord, he stood: All the while he made his supplication, he kneeled. His supplication being ended, he stood again to bless, as before, See v. 22.

*{with his hands spread up to heaven}* See v. 22.

**V. 55.** *{And he stood and blessed all the congregation of Israel}* As he began, so he ended with blessing the people. See v. 14.

*{with a loud voice, saying}* There was a great congregation: therefore he endeavored so to lift up his voice as all might hear him. Public duties are audibly to be uttered, Ps. 55.17.

**V. 56.** *{Blessed be the LORD}* Here blessing is to be taken for praising, Psal. 113.1,2.

*{that hath given rest unto his people Israel}* Rest is here opposed to all manner of public troubles. It comprises under it freedom from war, oppression and other evils, that use to arise either from enemies abroad, or from evil magistrates and tumultuous people at home. It implies peace, safety and security, Josh. 21.44. Act. 9.31. None but the Lord can give this, 2 Chron. 14.6.

*{according to all that he promised, there hath not failed one word of all his good promise}* Heb. *fallen*. i.e. God left not anything undone that he promised, but accomplished all to the full, Josh. 21.45. and 23.14,15.

*{which he promised by the hand of Moses his servant}* Or, *by the ministry of Moses*. See v. 53. This has relation to all the promises made to Abraham, Isaac, Jacob, and to their posterity, which are in sundry places of the five books of Moses set down: In special, it has relation to Deut. 12.10,11,12. Of this title servant see ch. 3.6.

**V. 57.** *{The LORD our God}* See v. 28.

*{be with us}* God's presence with his people is oft set forth under this phrase, and that by way of promise, as Deut. 31.6,8. Josh. 1.5. 1 Chron. 28.20. To these promises has Solomon here relation. Mark the inferences made upon those promises, Rom. 8.31. Heb. 13.5,6.

*{as he was with our fathers}* He means such pious ancestors as God had blessed from time to time.

*{let him not leave us nor forsake us}* This clause uses to be annexed to the former promise. It implies constancy in God's presence with his people, and in his blessings on them. God was with Saul; for *the Spirit of God came upon him*, 1 Sam. 11.6. and *God gave him another heart*, 1 Sam. 10.9. yet for his sin the Lord left him and forsook him, 1 Sam. 15.23,26. & 16.14. See 2 Sam. 7.15.

**V. 58.** *{That he may incline our hearts unto him}* Psal. 119.36. Otherwise nothing can be well done.

*{to walk in all his ways, and to keep his commandments, and his statutes, and his judgments}* See ch. 2.3.

*{as he commanded our fathers}* Gen. 17.1. Deut. 4.1. & 6.1. Josh. 24.14. 1 Sam. 7.3. & 12.24.

**V. 59.** *{And let these my words wherewith I have made supplication before the LORD}* In these words he does generally inculcate what in particular he had prayed for before, to show the earnestness of his desire.

*{be nigh}* i.e. be ever in his mind and memory. Let him show that he has respect thereunto.

*{day and night}* Continually.

*{that he maintain the cause}* See v. 45,49.

*{of his servant}* Solomon here intends himself, See v. 28. Though he were a great king, yet he scorns not the title of Gods servant. His father counted it a great honor, and pleads it before God, Psal. 116.16.

*{and the cause of his people Israel}* See v. 30.

*{at all times}* Heb. *the thing of a day in his day*. This phrase is oft used, and in different respects. It is translated *daily*, Exod. 5.13,19. Dan. 1.5. *every day*, Exod. 16.4. *upon his day*, Levit. 23.37. *as every dayes work required*, 2 Chron. 16.37. *after a certaine rate every day*, 2 Chronic. 8.13. *as the duty of every day required*, 2 Chronic. 8.13. Here it signifies *day after day*.

*{as the matter shall require}* A phrase of limitation, whereby he shows he desires no more than what may be just and equal. No pious man would have God to maintain an unjust cause.

**V. 60.** *{That all the people of the earth may know that the LORD is God, and that there is none else}* Deut. 4.39. Pious persons, even in the prayers they make for themselves, aim at the glory of God. They so desire mercy to be showed to them, and blessings conferred on them, as the Lord thereby may be acknowledged to be the only true God, and the fountain from whence all blessings flow forth to them.

**V. 61.** *{Let your heart therefore be perfect with the LORD your God}* He here means an entire heart subjecting itself in all sincerity wholly and only to God, 1 Chr. 28.9. & 29.19. 2 King. 20.3. This is the best and greatest perfection that in this world can be attained unto. See Gen. 17.1.

*{to walk in his statutes, and to keep his commandments}* See ch. 2.3.

*{as at this day}* Still from time to time continually, as now you do. See 1 Chr. 28.7.

**V. 62.** *{And the king and all Israel with him offered sacrifice before the LORD}* To Solomon's supplication sacrifices are added, both to testify his confidence of God's gracious acceptance, and also his thankful mind to God. For these were sacrifices of thanksgiving, Psal. 107.22. See v. 63.

**V. 63.** *{And Solomon offered}* Together with the priests, princes, and people, as 1 Chr. 15.11. & 29.23. & 30.24. & 35.7,8,9. Under these words, *all Israel*, in the former verse, priests, princes, and all under the king are comprised.

*{a sacrifice of peace-offerings which he offered unto the LORD}* See ch. 3.15. There were two ends of peace offerings; One to testify the hope which they had of their reconciliation with God; The other to testify their thankfulness. It was called a peace-offering, 1. Because God having bestowed some benefit upon them, for which this offering was made, he seemed to be appeased towards them. 2. Because upon apprehension thereof, the conscience of the offerer was pacified. 3. Because it was a kind of retribution, by returning thanks to God. 4. Because in offering of the same they desired and expected peace.

*{two and twenty thousand oxen, and an hundred and twenty thousand sheep}* Great sacrifices are mentioned, 1 Chr. 29.21. 2 Chr. 15.11. & 29.32. & 30.24. & 35.7,8,9. But none like to this. By multitude of sacrifices pious Jews were wont to testify their zealous and grateful affection toward God. Herein hypocrites would make show of imitating them, Mic. 6.7.

*{so the king and all the children of Israel}* They all brought oxen and sheep, and so made up the foresaid number. Zealous spirits are not satisfied with the duties of piety which they perform themselves, unless they bring thereunto all under their charge. And pious subjects will be ready to join with their sovereign in solemn and sacred duties.

*{dedicated the house of the LORD}* By their joint prayers, praises and sacrifices they set apart the temple to sacred uses.

**V. 64.** *{The same day did the king}* By the ministry of priests, 2 Chr. 7.7. For the king might not himself go into that court.

*{hallow}* Set apart to that holy service, Exod. 13.1,12. By such rites as were appointed by the law.

*{the middle of the court that was before the house of the LORD}* This was the priests' court, wherein they did either erect other altars for that present use, or used the pavement instead of an altar. See on Judg. 18.4.

*{for there he offered burnt-offerings and meat-offerings, and the fat of the peace offerings}* Under these three heads all ordinary sacrifices are comprised, as Josh. 22.23. Of burnt offerings see Lev. 1. Of meat offerings, Lev. 2. Of peace offerings, Lev. 3. See also ch. 3.15. He expressly mentions the fat of peace-offerings, because all the fat thereof was in an especial manner the Lord's, Levit. 3.16.

*{because the brasen altar that was before the LORD, was too little to receive the burnt-offerings and meat-offerings, and the fat of the peace-offerings}* This reason shows that this was an extraordinary case, even a case of necessity: and in such a case an ordinary course may be dispensed with, Numb. 9.11. 2 Chron. 29.34. & 30.2,3. Matt. 12.3,4. Of the brazen altar see 2 Chron. 4.1. This is said to stand before the Lord, because it was set in the priests' court directly before the most holy place and the ark therein. See 2 King. 19.14.

**V. 65.** *{And at that time}* At the dedication of the temple.

*{Solomon held a feast}* A feast for this very work, besides the feast of tabernacles, v. 2. see ch. 3.15. & 1 Chron. 16.3.

*{and all Israel with him}* As many as were then gathered together which were very many.

*{a great congregation}* For they came from all parts of the land, as the words following show.

*{from the entering in of Hamath}* Hamath was the uttermost part of Canaan on the north, Numb. 13.22,23. & 34.7,8.

*{unto the river of Egypt}* This was on the south, Numb. 34.3,5. Josh. 13.3. see ch. 4.21. These two coasts show they came from all parts of that nation, from north to south; under which by a synecdoche the east and west also are to be understood.

*{before the LORD our God}* He means before the temple and the most holy place wherein the ark was: Or this phrase may be used in regard of the manner of their keeping the feast: It was as in the presence of the Lord, after a holy spiritual manner.

*{seven days, and seven days, even fourteen days}* The first seven were for the dedication, 2 Chr. 7.9. The other seven for the feast of tabernacles. For that feast began on the fifteenth day of the month, Levit. 23.34. see 1 Chr. 15.26.

**V. 66.** *{On the eighth day}* This was the day next after the second feast, being the three and twentieth day of the second month, 2 Chr. 7.10. For on the two and twentieth day a holy convocation was enjoined, Levit. 23.36. Whereas it is said that on the eighth day there should be a holy convocation.

*{he sent the people away}* He dismissed the congregation, and gave them all liberty to go to their own houses.

*{and they blessed the king}* Or *thanked him*. Yea, and prayed to God for him, and that with a joyful acclamation.

*{and went unto their tents}* By tents he means their houses: but herein alludes to the habitations of Israel in the wilderness, which was in tents. See ch. 12.16.

*{joyful and glad of heart}* Not only their bodies were refreshed with the feast, but their spirits also were much cheered with Solomon's prayer and blessing, and the accomplishment of all things about the temple.

*{for all the goodness that the LORD had done for David his servant}* See ch. 3.6. Namely, in giving him so wise a son, who had accomplished what God had foretold concerning the temple, 2 Sam. 7.13. in establishing his throne, settling a good peace, and giving hope of the continuance of all.

*{and for Israel his people}* This is added because the benefit of all redounded to all Israel.

# CHAPTER 9

**Ver. 1.** *{And it came to pass when Solomon had finished the building of the house of the LORD}* And had also dedicated the same after that solemn manner set down in the former chapter, For v. 3. God says, *I have heard thy prayer*.

*{And the king’s house, &c.}* The latter part of this verse must be included in a parenthesis. To make up the sense of the latter part a principal verb may be repeated, and thus added, *He finished the Kings house, &c.* Or otherwise, all that appertains to the vision expressed in the verses following, from the beginning of v. 2. to v. 10. may be included in a parenthesis. Thus the tenth verse will depend on the first. And because there is so large a narration of the vision inserted, the words of the first verse are repeated in the tenth, which is usual after a long parenthesis. Under the Kings house he comprises the house of Lebanon, the queen's house, and other buildings mentioned ch. 7.2.

*{And all Solomon’s desire which he was pleased to do}* Whatsoever Solomon thought meet to build for his own or others' use, or for delight (whereof he gives some intimation Eccl. 2.4, &c.) he accomplished.

**V. 2.** *{That the LORD appeared to Solomon}* These words, as here translated, have relation to the first clause of the former verse, as if they had been thus joined together, *And it came to passe when Solomon had finished the building of the house of the Lord, that the Lord appeared, &c.* In the Hebrew there is a copulative particle, *And the Lord appeared*, which may be turned into a causative particle, and so the vision will be brought in as a reason why Solomon, having built the house of the Lord, built his own house, and what else pleased him, namely, because he confidently rested on God's promise to establish the throne of his Kingdom, &c. v. 5.

*{the second time}* This shows that there was no other appearance of God betwixt that ch. 3.5. and this.

*{as he had appeared unto him at Gibeon}* In a dream by night, really, evidently. See ch. 3.5. &c. 2 Chr. 7.12.

**V. 3.** *{And the LORD said unto him, I have heard thy prayer}* This shows that this vision was anon after Solomon's solemn prayer at the dedication of the temple. It may be the very night following, as he did the first time that he appeared to him, even the night after he had offered up his solemn sacrifices, ch. 3.4,5. It is not probable that God would defer his approbation of that prayer thirteen years after it was made. For it was thirteen years after that, before Solomon had built all he desired. This mention of God's hearing his prayer, gives instance of God's readiness to hear the prayer of his servants, Psal. 65.2. Dan. 9.23.

*{and thy supplication that thou hast made before me}* See ch. 8.28.

*{I have hallowed this house which thou hast built}* I have set it apart to those holy uses for which you have prayed, Gen. 2.3.

*{to put my name there for ever}* See ch. 8.13,16.

*{and mine eyes}* See ch. 8.29.

*{and my heart shall be there perpetually}* I will not only take notice of the prayers made, and services done in this house, but also set my heart thereon, and manifest my grace and favor therein. What I do there, shall be heartily done in love and goodwill.

**V. 4.** *{And if thou wilt}* See ch. 8.25.

*{walk before me}* See ch. 2.4.

*{as David thy father walked}* See ch. 3.14.

*{in integrity of heart}* Integrity implies the soundness of the whole heart; when the heart is carried one way, and no part thereof a contrary way, as 2 King. 17.41. & Zeph. 1.5. See Job 2.3. & 27.5. Psal. 16.1,11. Prov. 20.7.

*{and in uprightness}* See ch. 3.6.

*{to do}* The inward disposition of the heart must be manifested by the outward conversation of life.

*{according to all that I have commanded thee}* Obedience to God must be universal, not by halves, as 1 Sam. 15.8,9. and the rule thereof must be God's commandment. See 2 Kings 23.25. Luk. 1.6. Deut. 18.1.

*{and will keep my statutes, and my judgments}* See ch. 2.3.

**V. 5.** *{Then I will establish the throne of thy kingdom upon Israel for ever}* i.e. You and your posterity shall continue time after time to be kings over Israel, so as no other stock but yours shall sit on that throne. See ch. 2.4. & 8.25. 2 Sam. 7.16.

*{for ever}* See ch. 8.13.

*{as I promised to David}* 2 Sam. 7.11. 1 Chr. 22.10. God who promises, is faithful, Heb. 10.23.

*{thy father, saying}* See ch. 6.12.

*{There shall not fail thee a man upon the throne of Israel}* See ch. 8.25.

**V. 6.** *{But if you shall}* Failing on man's part to observe the condition annexed to God's promise, makes it void, 1 Sam. 2.30.

*{at all turn}* Heb. *turning turn*. Clean turn away, obstinately, totally.

*{from following me}* Heb. *from after me*. If you shall renounce me and my service; as servants who are weary of their master, and will no longer follow him, ch. 18.21.

*{you or your children}* i.e. either you to whom in particular the promise is made, or any of your children in any age after you. Where the condition is broken there will the forfeiture be taken.

*{and will not keep}* Omission of duty makes men liable to judgment; especially if obstinacy be added thereto, Lev. 16.14. Matth. 23.37.

*{my commandments and my statutes}* See ch. 2.3.

*{which I have set before you}* i.e. have made them known to you, that they might be to you as a way to walk in.

*{but go and serve}* Neglect of God's commandment is a forerunner of idolatry; and idolatry, of destruction.

*{other gods}* Whatsoever besides Jehovah has that respect yielded to it which is proper to God, is made another god, Exod. 10.3.

*{and worship them}* To serve implies in general all manner of obedience, or performing of anything in reference to superiority. To worship signifies an outward bowing of the body by way of adoration in acknowledgment of a deity. These two words, serve and worship, applied to the true or a false god, do oft intend one and same thing.

**V. 7.** *{Then will I cut off Israel out of the land which I have given them}* The metaphor of cutting off is taken from the husbandman's dealing with barren and dead trees. He stocks them up, root and branches, as Deut. 20.20. Judg. 6.28. Luke 13.7,9. Thus was Jeroboam dealt withal, ch. 14.10. and sundry other kings of Israel: yea, and all Israel itself, 2 King. 17.20.

*{and this house}* 2 King. 25.9.

*{which I have hallowed for my name}* See v. 3. Jer. 7.14.

*{will I cast out of my sight}* i.e. out of my favor and protection. This judgment is directly contrary to the blessing promised v. 3. Sin incenses God's wrath against the most sacred external evidences of his presence, and means of his worship. He will rather suffer them to be demolished, than his name to be dishonored by hypocrites or profane persons or idolaters in the use of them.

*{and Israel shall be a proverb}* i.e. a usual scoff or scorn, such as this, a very Jew, or, worse then a Jew.

*{and a by-word}* The Hebrew word signifies *a sharp or biting word*; such a scornful speech as pierces to the heart.

*{among all people}* As in the time of the Babylonish captivity the Jews were a scorn to all nations, so much more since the last destruction of their city and temple by the Romans. To other fearful judgments such shame and disgrace has been added as never the like to any other nation, Deut. 28.37. Jer. 24.9.

**V. 8.** *{And at this house which is high}* Under this epithet high all the external glory of the temple is comprised: and it is premised as the reason why people were so astonished at the destruction thereof; namely, because so great glory was turned into so vile contempt.

*{every one}* Friend and foe, homeborn and stranger, great and mean, young and old, all of all sorts.

*{shall be astonished}* Wondering at the judgment of God; some through much grief thereat, others through mere amazement.

*{and shall hiss}* A gesture of great scorn, spoken of malignant enemies.

*{and they shall say, Why hath the LORD done thus unto this land and unto this house?}* Deut. 29.24. Jer. 22.8. This is added as the effect and evidence of their astonishment.

**V. 9.** *{And they shall answer, because they forsook}* The very heathen detest apostasy as justly deserving severe vengeance. See Jer. 2.11. Lam. 4.15.

*{the LORD their God}* See ch. 8.28.

*{who brought forth their fathers out of the land of Egypt}* Former deliverances and mercies much aggravate apostasy.

*{and have taken hold}* The Hebrew word signifies a fast holding of a thing so as not to let it go, Exod. 9.2. Job 18.9. Thus they who forsake the true God, hold fast their false gods.

*{upon other gods, and have worshipped them and served them}* See v. 6.

*{therefore the LORD brought upon them all this evil}* God's judgments on unworthy professors appear just even in all men's eyes.

**V. 10.** *{And it came to pass, &c.}* Here he returns to Solomon's proceeding about his buildings: And for better passing thereunto, he repeats part of the first verse. See on v. 1.

*{at the end of twenty years}* This account is to be taken from the beginning of the temple's building, not of Solomon's reign: For that which here is spoken of was in the four and twentieth year of his reign, ch. 6.1.

*{when Solomon had built the two houses}* And other houses joined unto them, mentioned ch. 7, &c.

*{the house of the LORD}* In seven years, ch. 6.38.

*{and the king’s house}* In thirteen years, ch. 7.1.

**V. 11.** *{Now Hiram the king of Tyre}* See ch. 5.1.

*{had furnished Solomon with cedar-trees and fir-trees}* See ch. 5.10.

*{and with gold}* See v. 14.

*{according to all his desire}* See ch. 5.10.

*{that then Solomon gave Hiram twenty cities}* This recompense Solomon gave to Hiram for the timber and gold mentioned before, and for the pains and workmanship which Hiram's servants took and did about Solomon's work, ch. 5.9, &c. besides the wheat and oil which he gave him, ch. 5.11.

*{in the land of Galilee}* Styled Galilee of the Gentiles, Isa. 9.1. Mat. 4.15. because before David's time it was inhabited of the Gentiles, and because it bordered upon the Gentiles, namely, upon Tyrus. Or Galilee may be taken appellatively, for a circuit of land, Joel 3.4. and appertained to such cities as were without the land of Israel's inheritance, which might not be alienated.

**V. 12.** *{And Hiram came out from Tyre to see the cities which Solomon had given him}* That he might the better consider their situation, and other commodities appertaining to them.

*{and they pleased him not}* Heb. *were not right in his eyes*. They gave him no content: therefore he returned them back again to Solomon. 2 Chron. 8.2. See on Josh. 19.27.

**V. 13.** *{And he said}* He plainly expressed his mind, and did not conceal his discontent.

*{what cities are these which thou hast given me}* This interrogation imports a great dislike.

*{my brother}* This is spoken by one king to another: For kings are brothers in office, ch. 20.32.

*{and he called them the land of Cabul}* i.e. displeasing, or dirty. For they were in low, moorish places.

*{unto this day}* This phrase implies a perpetual continuance of a thing, as ch. 10.12. & 12.19. Gen. 26.33. at least a continuance of it till the time of writing this history.

**V. 14.** *{And Hiram sent to the king}* Upon compact; and that time after time, while Solomon was building the foresaid houses, and not all at once.

*{sixscore talents of gold}* The Jews had two talents, the one sacred, the other common. The sacred was in weight and worth as much more as the common. The sacred in gold was reputed to be three thousand seven hundred and fifty pound in value; the common, one thousand eight hundred seventy and five pounds sterling. Multiply these one hundred and twenty times, and the value will be found to be very great, namely, of the sacred, four hundred and fifty thousand; and of the common, two hundred twenty and five thousand pounds. See on 1 Chron. 22.14.

**V. 15.** *{And this is the reason of the levy which king Solomon raised}* This levy was especially of men, as ch. 5.13. For he had such plenty of money, and of all things fit to accomplish great works, as he might well want men to finish the same, and thereupon made great levies of men. This very word is applied v. 21. to such men as he used for his works. There might also levies be made of money. See ch. 12.4.

*{for to build the house of the LORD}* And all things appertaining thereunto.

*{and his own house}* See v. 5.

*{and Millo}* According to the Hebrew notation it signifies *fullness*, or *a place filled up*. Some therefore take it for a high strong bulwark filled up with earth. Others for a spacious place for people that attended the king's court to meet in. It is called a house 2 King. 12.20. It appears to have been a house of defense, or a fort; because in danger of enemies, Hezekiah repaired it, 2 Chr. 32.5. It was in Zion which David won from the Jebusites, 2 Sam. 5.7,9. Solomon both here, and ch. 11.27. is said to build it, in that he so repaired and beautified it as it seemed to be new.

*{and the wall of Jerusalem}* See ch. 3.1. The re-edifying of this wall Neh. 3. shows how large it was. Such was the benefit of it, as upon the repairing of it there was a solemn dedication, Neh. 12.27, &c.

*{and Hazor}* This city was in the tribe of Naphtali, Josh. 19.32,36. See Josh. 11.10, &c.

*{and Megiddo}* A city in Manasseh, Josh. 17.11. Judg. 1.27. Here Josiah received his mortal wound, 2 Chr. 35.22.

*{and Gezer}* In Ephraim, belonging to the Levites, Josh. 21.21. 1 Chr. 6.67.

**V. 16.** *{For Pharaoh king of Egypt had gone up}* Here he shows how Gezer became Solomon's.

*{and taken Gezer}* For the Canaanites and Philistines held it at that time, Judg. 1.29. 1 Sam. 27.8. 1 Chr. 20.4.

*{and burnt it with fire}* It is probable that the Canaanites stood out so obstinately against Pharaoh as he was forced to set it on fire.

*{and slain the Canaanites that dwelt in the city}* This he did, lest they should at any time get head, mutiny, and seek to recover their right again.

*{and given it for a present unto his daughter, Solomon’s wife}* It was the more acceptable present, because it lay within the confines of Israel, Josh. 21.21.

**V. 17.** *{And Solomon built Gezer}* For it was burnt, v. 16.

*{and Beth-horon the nether}* Two Beth-horons, the upper and nether, are bounded, Josh. 16.3,5. The nether was in Benjamin, Josh. 18.11,13. The upper in Ephraim, Josh. 16.5. and appertained to the Levites, Josh. 21.22. It is said 2 Chron. 8.5. that he also built Beth-horon the upper; so as here one is put for both synecdochically.

**V. 18.** *{And Baalath}* In Dan, Josh. 19.44.

*{and Tadmor}* In the coast of Syria.

*{in the wilderness}* He means a dry and sandy place.

*{in the land}* Namely of Israel, though at the uttermost coasts thereof. This clause in the land may be referred to all the forementioned places.

**V. 19.** *{And all the cities of store that Solomon had}* i.e. cities wherein ammunition, provision, and all manner of preparation for war, navigation, food, clothing, and other necessaries were laid up, as Ex. 1.11.

*{and cities for his chariots, and cities for his horsemen}* These chariots and horsemen were both for his royal use in time of peace, and also for necessary use in times of war. For it is an especial point of prudence, in time of peace to provide for war, 2 King. 8.21. see 2 Chr. 18.4. ch. 5. & 22.31.

*{and that which Solomon desired}* Heb. *the desire of Solomon which he desired*. He had in abundance all manner of timber and stone, and other materials, and abundance of money, and men skillful in all manner of workmanship, and store of laborers for inferior services, and excellent wisdom in himself to order and direct all, and God's blessing upon all; so that he might well accomplish the desire which he desired.

*{to build in Jerusalem}* See chap. 7.1, &c. What he there built.

*{and in Lebanon}* Other places besides that fair edifice, described ch. 7.3,4,5. are here meant: for that was in Jerusalem, these in Lebanon.

*{and in all the land of his dominion}* All the cities mentioned v. 15,17,18, and elsewhere.

**V. 20.** *{And all the people that were left of the Amorites, Hittites, Perizzites, Hivites, and Jebusites, which were not of the children of Israel}* See Judg. 1.21,27,29,30,31,33. & 2.23.

**V. 21.** *{Their children that were left after them in the land}* Here he shows whom he meant v. 20. namely the posterity of those heathen there mentioned that lived in Solomon's time in the land of Israel.

*{whom the children of Israel also were not able utterly to destroy}* This disability arose partly from their own negligence, Judg. 1.21,27, &c. and partly from the wise providence of God, who left them to prove Israel; Judg. 2.22.

*{upon those did Solomon levy a tribute of bond-service}* This does exemplify the levy before mentioned, v. 15. & ch. 5.13.

*{unto this day}* See v. 13. These strangers proved proselytes, and were incorporated into the state of Israel, as the Gibeonites, who are called Nethinims, 1 Chr. 9.2. Ezr. 2.43. Solomon imposing a tax or service on them and their posterity, they continued in Israel very many generations, and carried the name of Solomons servants, Ezra 2.55. Neh. 7.57.

**V. 22.** *{But of the children of Israel did Solomon make no bondmen}* Such respect did God bear to the seed of Abraham, with whom he had entered into covenant, Gen. 17.7. as he instituted a law against making any of them bondslaves, Lev. 25.39. Solomon therefore in a tender respect to his countrymen, and in a dutiful respect to God's law, would make none of them bondmen.

*{but they were}* Though Solomon exempted the Israelites from villainage, yet not from all employments. He would not have them trained up to idleness.

*{men of war}* Trained up to that noble discipline. For a soldier's calling is both warrantable and honorable.

*{and his servants}* They were employed in free and honorable functions, ordained by Solomon. These are opposed to bondmen.

*{and his princes}* Such as had dignity and authority conferred on them. They were magistrates and governors in the commonwealth.

*{and his captains}* Commanders in war.

*{and rulers of his chariots, and his horsemen}* Such as we call masters of the king's horse: such as had command over the king's equerry, over the grooms of his stables, and over his chariot men. See ch. 11.28. Of chariots see v. 19.

**V. 23.** *{These were the chief of the officers that were over Solomon’s work}* He means such Israelites as were before mentioned.

*{five hundred & fifty which bare rule over the people}* 2 Chron. 8.10. Mention is made but of two hundred and fifty. But five hundred did serve by turns, two hundred and fifty at a time; and these only are intended in that place. The odd fifty were over the rest to take account of them as they served in their course. All these reckoned up together make the number of five hundred and fifty. Or 2 Chron. 8.10. such officers may be spoken of as had power over people in the commonwealth; but here such as were over workmen in Solomon's buildings and other works, whereabout the inspection of more overseers was requisite. Whereas ch. 5.16. mention is made of three thousand and three hundred officers over the work; I answer that they were over the work of the temple, whereto Solomon had a far greater care than of his own house; and therefore had more workmen and more officers thereabout. See on ch. 7.1.

**V. 24.** *{Pharaoh’s daughter came up}* Having finished a royal house fit for such a princess, as he intended long before, ch. 3.1. he now brings her into it. Another special reason for his bringing her out of the house of David, see 2 Chron. 8.11.

*{out of the city of David}* See ch. 2.10. unto her house which Solomon had built for her. See ch. 7.8.

*{then did he build Millo}* See v. 15.

**V. 25.** *{And three times in a year did Solomon offer}* At the three great feasts, 1. the passover, 2. the feast of weeks, 3. and the feast of tabernacles, Deut. 16.16. It appears 2 Chro. 8.13. that Solomon observed other feasts, and offered sacrifices at sundry other times. But these he observed most solemnly and constantly. And by a synecdoche other feasts and sacrifices are comprised under these.

*{burnt-offerings and peace-offerings}* See ch. 3.15.

*{upon the altar which he built unto the LORD}* i.e. the great brazen altar, ch. 8.64. See 2 Chro. 4.1.

*{and he burnt}* Namely, by the ministry of the priests: by whose ministry also he offered the forementioned offering. For it was not lawful for the king or any other but priests, to offer either incense or other offering. See 2 Chr. 26.16.

*{incense}* Incense was a sweet perfume made of four odoriferous spices, whereof see Exod. 30.34. It was such as none might make according to the composition thereof, upon pain of being cut off, Exod. 30.37,38. It is styled *sweet incense*, Exod. 25.6. and *sweet odors*, Levit. 26.31. in a double respect; Of the external savor, it being exceeding sweet; and of the typical effect, which was to cause a sweet savor in God's nostrils. 1. It was every day, morning and evening, burnt in God's house, Exod. 30.8,9. that God might take delight to dwell there. 2. It was joined to all sorts of sacrifices, Psal. 66.15. to make them acceptable. 3. It was offered up when God's wrath was incensed, Numb. 16.46. to pacify the same. 4. It used to be burning while the people were praying, Luke 1.10. to procure audience. 5. It was carried with blood into the most holy place, Lev. 16.11,12,13. to make the atonement effectual. The incense did typify Christ, who was most precious, 1 Pet. 2.7. yea, as the spices bruised, Isa. 53.5. and made *a sacrifice for a sweet smelling savour*, Ephes. 5.2. *He maketh continual intercession for us*, Rom. 8.34. Heb. 7.25. In him *God is well pleased*, Matt. 3.17. and by him is his wrath pacified, Isa. 32.2. and an atonement made, Rom. 5.11. Incense did also morally set out prayer, which coming from *a broken and contrite heart*, Psal. 51.17. with faith, Mar. 11.24. and fervor, Jam. 5.16. laid on Christ our altar and censer, Heb. 13.10. Joh. 16.23. *ascendeth into heaven*, 2 Chr. 30.27. and is accepted of God, Job 42.8.

*{upon}* Heb. *upon it*.

*{the altar that was before the LORD}* See ch. 6.22.

*{so he finished the house}* This has relation to v. 1.

**V. 26.** *{And king Solomon made a navy of ships in Ezion-geber, which is beside Eloth}* These were two places in the wilderness, where the Israelites had stations, Deut. 2.8. and both havens of the Red Sea.

*{on the shore}* Heb. *lip*.

*{of the red sea, in the land of Edom}* i.e. on that part of the Red Sea which coasted on the land of Edom.

**V. 27.** *{And Hiram sent in the navy his servants}* The navy was Solomon's, and the ships made by his appointment. Therefore whereas it is said 2 Chr. 8.18. That *Hiram sent Solomon, by the hands of his servants, ships*, the meaning is that Hiram guided Solomon's ships by the skill and industry of his servants. For the word translated sent signifies also *to conduct, guide, or bring on the way*, Gen. 18.16.

*{ship-men that had knowledge of the sea}* Tyre was an island in the sea, and many of the inhabitants thereof were from their youth trained up to be mariners and pilots.

*{with the servants of Solomon}* Hiram's servants did but assist Solomon's men.

**V. 28.** *{And they came to Ophir}* Ophir is an island in the Indies, where the most precious gold is to be had. Hereupon the finest gold is styled *gold of Ophir*, Job 22.24. & 28.16. Psal. 45.9. Isa. 13.12. It is also styled *gold of Parvaim*, 2 Chr. 3.6. which is supposed to be in Havilah, Gen. 2.11. It is called *perfections of gold*, 2 Chr. 4.21. *pure gold*, ch. 10.21. *fine gold*, 2 Chr. 3.5,8. and *refined gold*, 1 Chr. 28.18.

*{and fet from thence gold}* Solomon had better success than Jehoshaphat, ch. 22.48.

*{four hundred and twenty talents}* 2 Chr. 8.18. mention is made of four hundred and fifty talents. R. Four hundred and fifty were brought to Solomon; the thirty overplus were for all manner of charges. Of talents see v. 14. Four hundred and twenty talents amount to a million five hundred seventy-five thousand pounds.

*{and brought it to king Solomon}* This was an especial means of increasing Solomon's treasure, and enabling him to do so great works as he did. For not once only, but time after time, did Solomon thus receive gold. See ch. 10.22.

# CHAPTER 10

**Ver. 1.** *{And when the queen of Sheba}* 2 Chr. 9.1. Luk. 11.31. Sheba was a country south from Jerusalem, far remote, in the utmost parts of the earth, Matth. 12.42. situated in Arabia the Happy. It afforded store of gold, sweet spices, and precious stones, Psal. 72.15. Ezek. 27.22. See v. 2.

*{heard of the fame of Solomon}* His wisdom was famous throughout the world, v. 24. ch. 4.31.

*{concerning the name of the LORD}* The report went (as the truth was, ch. 3.12.) that the Lord had inspired him with more than ordinary wisdom. Yea further, by this phrase is implied that desire of the knowledge of Jehovah, and of that divine wisdom which she believed to be in Solomon, put her on to undertake this long journey. And herein she is to be commended far above Plato, Pythagoras, and others, that went into remote countries for philosophy's sake.

*{she came}* Her sex, her dignity, her wealth, the remoteness of her abode, the occasion of her coming, the fame of Solomon, and the end thereof, the name of the Lord, do all commend her example. It is said of many that they came to hear the wisdome of Solomon, v. 24. ch. 4.34. but the distinct history of none is set down but of this queen: For her example excelled all others.

*{to prove him}* i.e. to get evidence of his wisdom, that so she herself might be made wiser thereby, Pro. 1.5. & 13.20.

*{with hard questions}* The Hebrew word is translated *dark speeches*, Num. 12.8. *dark sentences*, Dan. 8.23. *Proverbs*, Hab. 2.6. *riddles*, Judg. 14.12. They were doubtless questions about weighty matters, and so intricate and difficult as all the heathen philosophers, astrologers, magicians, and other learned men could not resolve.

**V. 2.** *{And she came to Jerusalem with a very great train}* A multitude of attendance, called her servants, v. 13. is comprised under this word train. See Gen. 50.9. Her retinue was answerable to her state at home. Her royal attendance, coming so long a journey, much magnified her zeal. She spared not pains, nor cost. Of Jerusalem see ch. 3.15.

*{with camels}* These beasts could endure long and hard travel, and bear great burdens. They had long necks, long legs, and large bodies, which gave occasion to sundry proverbs, as Matth. 19.24. & 23.24. They were much used of old, Gen. 24.10. & 30.43. & 31.17. Judg. 6.5. 1 Sam. 30.17. Job 1.3. & 42.12.

*{that bare spices, and very much gold, and precious stones}* See v. 1.

*{and when she was come unto Solomon, she communed with him of all that was in her heart}* Which she had either before purposed, or then came to her mind. The phrase of communing with imports, that as she put forth her own wit to the uttermost to make the more thorough proof of Solomon's wisdom, so also she made the best advantage she could to herself by diligence hearkening thereunto.

**V. 3.** *{And Solomon told her}* He resolved and expounded her doubts, or such obscure and difficult cases as she propounded.

*{all her questions}* Heb. *words*.

*{there was not any thing hid from the king which he told her not}* As this gives proof of that incomparable wisdom which Solomon had, ch. 3.12. & 4.30,31. so it gives evidence of his readiness to communicate it to others.

**V. 4.** *{And when the queen of Sheba had seen}* i.e. well observed, weighed and considered.

*{all Solomon’s wisdom}* The effects and evidences of it.

*{and the house that he had built}* Here especially is meant the house of the Lord, with all the parts and courts thereof, with other excellent things appertaining thereto, so far as by a stranger might be seen: but withal the other fair houses that he built are also intended: for certainly she saw all.

**V. 5.** *{And the meat of his table}* That variety and plenty of dishes which was set upon his table, and the discreet ordering of all.

*{and the sitting of his servants}* Sitting is a word of dignity and honor, opposed to standing. For by servants he means counselors of state, peers, and other chief officers, who all were servants to the king, yet opposed to ministers or attendants.

*{and the attendance}* Heb. *standing*.

*{of his ministers}* These waited; and that they might be ready to go whither they were appointed, and do what they were commanded, they were standing. This sets forth their condition, they were ministering servants; and their disposition, they were willing and ready to do what was commanded.

*{and their apparel}* This was ordered according to their degrees and places. For one end of apparel is to distinguish sexes, degrees, callings, and other differences. See 2 Chr. 9.4.

*{and his cup-bearers}* Or *butlers*. In regard of the plenty and variety of plate, bread, and all manner of drink, which they set out, and their manner of ordering all, great admiration was worked in her. Comely and due performance of service in a family does commend not only the prudence of servants, but of their masters also.
